Liam Rosenior says Chelsea sacking had 'nothing to do with football' as he raises questions about 'tolerance' in England in first revelations about being axed just three months into the job

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Published: 06:37 EDT, 5 November 2026 | Updated: 18:17 EDT, 5 November 2026

Liam Rosenior has claimed he was treated unfairly during his doomed 106 days in charge of Chelsea, and that the way it ended in disaster had ‘nothing to do with football’.

Rosenior was relieved of his duties after losing five consecutive games without scoring. The fifth defeat in that run – a 3-0 loss at Brighton in which Chelsea did not even muster a shot on target – saw the players accused of downing tools to get rid of their head coach. He lasted 106 days in total.

However, Rosenior, now manager of Paris FC, who are fourth in France’s Ligue 1, has claimed his Stamford Bridge sacking was 'nothing to do with football' in a cryptic comment about 'tolerance' in English football.

The 42-year-old Englishman took aim at ‘the media’ in particular as he said in an interview with So Foot: ‘It was the best experience of my life, and the hardest, too. It was fantastic, even though it only lasted four months. I don’t want to go into details now. I’ll talk about it one day, when the time is right. But in a country where tolerance is an important value, what happened there has nothing to do with football.’

Pressed to explain, Rosenior continued: ‘I know, I answered without answering it. Actually, the moment that worried me the most was at the beginning, when we went through that five-game winning streak.

Liam Rosenior was seen passing a note to Alejandro Garnacho with Chelsea 8-2 down on aggregate against PSG - and the reaction to it clearly still rankles with him

Then-Chelsea manager Rosenior was compared to The Office star David Brent online

‘Any club would have been happy with such a run, but not Chelsea. You get good results and you’re in the spotlight. Then, there was a kind of snowball effect in the media.

‘They made fun of those instructions I gave (Alejandro) Garnacho on a scrap of paper when we were down to 10 men against PSG (during the second leg in March as they lost 8-2 on aggregate).

'The clothes I wore became a joke, the way I spoke, too. I’m not a joke! You’re the first people I’m talking to about this, and probably the last for a long time, but one day, I’ll tell you what really happened.’

It is unclear whether by ‘the media’, Rosenior meant those reporters who covered the club or social media, where he was routinely mocked and referred to as ‘LinkedIn Liam’.

Those within the actual Chelsea pack who attended Rosenior’s press conferences believe he was treated kindly despite the Blues boss not helping himself at times.

For example, his decision to suddenly drop Robert Sanchez for Filip Jorgensen backfired, especially in their Champions League last-16 first-leg loss away at PSG. When Jorgensen was then injured, Sanchez returned to the starting line-up with his confidence having been dented.

Rosenior also created a stir when he argued with a member of Arsenal’s support staff for encroaching in Chelsea’s half during their warm-up before the second leg of their Carabao Cup semi-final. He accused his opponents of breaking the ‘etiquettes in football’ afterwards.

After his players huddled around the referee Paul Tierney in the centre circle before a Premier League loss to Newcastle, Rosenior defended their pre-match routine. He described it as being designed to ‘respect the ball’ – words which were also ridiculed on social media, even if that idea did originate from Willie Isa, Chelsea’s hire from rugby league.

The former Strasbourg manager is now enjoying life back in France as manager of Paris FC

Rosenior added in his interview that he was never accepted in the Premier League, saying: ‘In England, there’s this preconceived notion of what a Premier League coach should be, how he should behave. You have to conform to that standard. People who know me know that I’m someone who tries to be sincere and authentic.

‘But I don’t have control over how the media describes me or talks about me. What I’ve understood now, and this is important for all coaches worldwide, is that social media, or the media in general, can dictate public opinion about the quality of a player or a coach.

‘This isn’t new, of course, but the biggest lesson from my time at Chelsea is knowing that they are also capable of constructing the entire narrative around a club’s trajectory.’

4 High-Protein Dessert Recipes That Taste Good—and Will Help You Hit Your Macros

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

As we know, protein is a crucial part of healthy eating and building muscle. Without adequate daily protein, the chances of packing on all the muscle you want are slim.

While the ideal amount of protein will vary from individual to individual, it’s a good idea to spread out your intake of this crucial macro throughout the day to make it easier to get all that you need. Trying to pack 100 grams of protein into a single meal is never a wise move. Who ever said that getting your fill of protein needs to stop at dinner? The protein spread can (and should) include dessert. With a little effort and know-how, you can give your ab-friendly sweat treats a protein boost without making them taste all healthy and what-not. Using the right ingredients, it’s certainly possible to make a dessert that gives you 10 grams or more of protein, which is significant in your daily pursuit to get enough. And, sure, you could just blend together a post-dinner protein shake, but where’s the fun in that?

Higher-protein desserts, be it for an end-of-day treat or part of a game day spread, can help you reach your goals for this clutch macronutrient. Here’s how to make some treats that do much more than satisfy a sweet tooth. Everything in moderation, right?

Certain ingredients can give your treats a protein boost. So if you’re looking for a dessert that’ll help you build muscle, include any of these protein heroes.

Protein Powder: Good for much more than your post-gym recovery shakes, versatile protein powder can be used in a huge variety of dessert recipes, both cooked and uncooked. For baking, it can replace some of the flour in a recipe. But it’s best to use plant-based protein powders in baking recipes like muffins and cakes as they give a much better texture than powders made from whey.

Peanut Butter Powder: This is the best thing to add nutty taste to dessert that you probably aren’t using. Peanut butter powder is made by pressing roasted peanuts to remove most of their natural oils, then grinding the defatted peanuts into a fine powder. This process significantly reduces the fat and calorie content compared to peanut butter, and results in a more protein-dense product. A good buy is Naked Nutrition Peanut Butter Powder.

Greek Yogurt: Enhance the protein content of your desserts by using Greek yogurt or Skyr instead of regular yogurt in recipes. It’s an easy substitution that packs an extra protein punch with minimal change to the flavor or texture.

Chia Seeds: Chia seeds offer four grams of protein in just two tablespoons —and they help create a creamy pudding that will be a delicious high-protein dessert (or breakfast).

These (mostly) healthy high protein desserts are sure to satisfy and will help ramp up your protein intake throughout the day.

In a blender or food processor, blend together the cottage cheese, maple syrup and vanilla until smooth. Transfer to a mixing bowl and stir in the oat flour, peanut butter powder, and cocoa powder until well incorporated. Store in an airtight container in the refrigerator. It should stay fresh for up to one week.

This protein-heavy, sweet bread is great for breakfast, dessert, or a snack. Sweet potato provides natural sweetness, while peanut butter adds richness and some additional protein to each slice. It’s great with a generous smear of cream cheese. Be sure to use a plant-based protein powder instead of whey for better texture.

Preheat oven to 350°F. Line a standard-sized loaf pan with parchment paper or use a non-stick silicone loaf pan.

In a large bowl, mix together flour, protein powder, baking powder, baking soda, cinnamon, and salt. In a separate bowl, whisk together sweet potato, peanut butter, milk, maple syrup, and vanilla. Add wet ingredients to bowl with dry ingredients and mix gently until everything is moist. Pour batter into pan and smooth the surface with a spatula. Sprinkle on peanuts and press them down lightly to adhere.

Bake for 40 minutes, or until a toothpick inserted into the center of the loaf comes out mostly clean. Allow loaf to cool in pan for 10 minutes before cooling the loaf further on a metal rack.

Here’s how to give your dessert a taste of the tropics. The superpower of chia seeds is that they soak up liquid like a sponge, which produces a pudding-like texture. This is also a stand-out make-ahead breakfast option. Greek yogurt adds plenty of protein, but you can go bigger on this macro by also blending in your favorite protein powder.

Place mango, milk, yogurt, honey, lime juice, lime zest, ginger and a pinch of salt in a blender and blend until smooth. Divide among 2 jars. Add half of the chia seeds to each jar and stir to combine. Sprinkle on almonds or pistachios. Seal shut and chill for at least 3 hours and up to 3 days.

Avocado gives these ice cream-like frozen cups a fudgy consistency and plenty of good nutrition. Protein powder adds a solid amount of the muscle-making macro, bananas are here for some natural sweetness, and cayenne provides as surprising spicy kick. Only if all ice cream could be this good for you and still taste great.

Place bananas, avocado flesh, nut butter, protein powder, maple syrup or honey, cocoa powder, cinnamon, cayenne, and a pinch of salt in a blender or food processor container and process until smooth.

Line muffin pan with 8 paper liners. (You can skip this step if using silicone muffin cups). Scoop banana batter into muffin cups. Sprinkle on flaky salt if using. Place tray in the freezer and freeze for at least 2 hours, or until solid. Unmold fudge pucks and store in the freezer in an airtight container until ready to serve. When ready to serve, leave fudge pucks out at room temperature for a few minutes to soften slightly.

Linehouse reimagines industrial aesthetics for Jen Hangzhou hotel in Kohn Pedersen Fox-designed building

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Photography by Jonathan Leijonhufvud and Wen Studio

International studio Linehouse has completed the interiors of the Jen hotel in Hangzhou, China, which features details that nod to local craft and the area’s industrial heritage.

The hotel, which is owned by the Shangri-La Hotel Group, is located along Hangzhou’s Dongxin River in an urban setting and flanked by two former industrial factory compounds.

The 18-floor hotel building was designed by US architecture firm Kohn Pedersen Fox, using steel, grey brick and glass to reference the site’s industrial past.

The same design approach was extended into the interior by Linehouse, which focuses on showcasing local craft and nods to the industrial elements of the former factories.

“Jen Hangzhou is an urban hotel that celebrates its neighbourhood context – one that layers history, craft, culture and rich materiality into a new perspective on Hangzhou,” Linehouse said.

The studio used earth-toned marble, oak panelling and relaxed furniture in the ground-floor lobby to create a calm and warm atmosphere.

At the entrance, guests are greeted by a textured artwork by artist Elsa Jeandedieu that depicts an abstract version of the bridges and canal close to the hotel.

Patchwork patterns on the marble floor and on a bespoke rug in the lobby were informed by the street layout and tile patterns of the surrounding neighbourhood.

All the hospitality spaces, including a restaurant, a bar and function rooms are located on the sixth floor.

The long, linear OpenHouse restaurant is wrapped by full-height glazing that invites natural light inside.

A timber design feature spans the ceiling and was informed by the craft of sunmao, a traditional timber joinery technique. It is highlighted by a thin black metal frame that supports suspended lighting.

At the centre of the restaurant, a coffee bar splits the space into a dining area on one side and an all-day lounge on the other.

In the private dining room, the timber canopy has an angled design and is decorated with burnt red and yellow hues. In the rest of the OpenHouse restaurant, rooms feature teal tiles and handmade ceramic wall lighting.

The adjacent Koan Bar is a triple-height venue characterised by an undulating black metal staircase that takes guests to a terrace on the upper floor, with incorporated platforms where guests can socialise.

The sinuous staircase is carved between walls lined with concrete panels, another nod to the site’s industrial heritage.

Beneath it, curved booths in walnut timber and tan leather provide intimate seating spaces.

Three interconnected spaces on the sixth floor, enveloped by undulating timber walls, will be used for events and intimate social gatherings. Like in the lobby, bespoke carpets feature patterns abstracted from urban details in the neighbourhood.

The seventh floor is dedicated to wellness and comprises a gym, a yoga room, a spa, a pool and changing rooms, made from tactile materials with muted tones to create a calm environment.

At the gym, a lava stone wall has engravings that illustrate movement, while the yoga room features hand-chiseled timber panels.

A sloped skylit annex accommodates the swimming pool.

The 176 guest rooms were conceived as “urban retreats”. Here, Linehouse used a restrained palette of gun metal, oak datum, textured render and tan leather to underline the industrial aesthetic.

“The public areas explore traditional craft techniques such as sunmao timber joinery, clay tiling and bricks, natural patterns, textures and renders, whilst the guestrooms explore a refined industrial aesthetic,” Linehouse said.

“Red brick, concrete, metal and bold forms reference the surrounding industrial environment whilst handcrafted tiles, vintage timber, colours and metals with different patinas introduce warmth, richness and texture.”

The guest rooms have curved ceilings that create indirect lighting and add softness to the space, while the bathrooms are illuminated by glass-block walls.

Founded by Alex Mok and Briar Hickling in 2013, Linehouse is an architecture and interior practice with studios in Shanghai, Hong Kong and Auckland.

It has recently designed Ink Ink Market in Shanghai with a translucent sloping facade, as well as the interiors for Bangkok’s Central Park shopping centre with three dramatic atriums, which was shortlisted of Retail Interior (large) by this year’s Dezeen Awards.

Area: 12,850 sqm Client: Kerry Properties Operator: Shangri-La Design principal: Alex Mok Design team: Wang Jue, Jingru Tong, Yunbin Lou, Cherngyu Chen, Inez Low, Yeling Guo, Aiwen Shao, Mia Zhou, Norman Wang, Yuechen Wu Lighting: LPA Decorative lighting: Kris Lite, Van Collection, Yi Design, Yun Zhao Studio, Fine Lumens, Santa & Cole, Audo, Timothy Oulton, La Nuit Design consultant: EDGE Design Interior contractor: Terart Decoration Design & Engineering Co., Ltd, Shenzhen Lead management consultant: Wong & Ouyang (HK) Ltd. LDI & structural consultant: Zhejiang Province Institute of Architectural Design and Research MEP engineer consultant: Hangzhou MJP Mechanical & Electrical Design Consultant Ltd.

Photography by Jonathan Leijonhufvud and Wen Studio

How Yoga Brings You Closer to Yourself and the Life You Want

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

In Yoga Journal’s Archives series, we share a curated collection of articles originally published in past issues beginning in 1975. This article about yoga as a science and an art first appeared in the January-February 1977 issue of Yoga Journal. Find more of our Archives here.

Yoga is a science, an art, and a technique which spans the multilevel functioning of the individual. It is a science because of its empirical approach, presented most completely by the classic Yoga Sutras of Patanjali at least 1,000 years ago. Science is defined as a methodology which requires empirical proof of its theories; it requires that which proves a theory be easily repeatable by another experimenter if he/she follows the rules of the experiment.

Yoga, by this definition, is indeed a science. Its rules are given in sutra form and the presentation of Patanjali is a “how-to” book of the highest order. The practitioner need only follow the lead of Patanjali in order to have his own personal empirical results. (It is true that the directions are in parts esoteric and apparently illusive; this points out the necessity of having a teacher upon whom the practioner can rely for advice during certain intervals.) In addition to the work by Patanjali, the Hatha Yoga Pradipika and the Gheranda and Shiva Samhita are references which can explain the science of yoga.

But yoga is an art, too. Individuals have dedicated their lives to the perfection of this art. This does not necessarily imply the production of tangible output such as texts, although these have been produced in the history of traditional Indian thought. Rather, the use of the word “art” in this context implies the movement toward perfection within, the embodiment of beauty as it can be expressed through the human being. If one dedicates his/her life to the art of yoga, this implies a dedication which does not involve merely daily practice numbering a few hours. Instead, it implies a minute by minute practice which spans years, and some believe, lifetimes of existence.

The art of yoga is the practice of yoga on all levels of existence; this means every thought, action, dream, relationship, food intake, sexual activity, occupation, etc., are brought into the wider perspective of observation in light of the individual’s desire to understand the Self. This understanding does not imply intellectual understanding alone. It implies the “living” or the “being” in the Self rather than just mentally knowing about the Self. One can compare this to the experience of being in a hurricane with merely reading about the experience of a hurricane; the effect would be quite different.

One who lives in the Self has come into a wholeness of being, possibly for us all by virtue of our human existence, but available presently to only a handful due a complex interplay of events which, for whatever reasons, has brought them to a newer threshold of consciousness. Thus the art of yoga lies in bringing perfection into action through becoming one with oneself.

Part of the ability to do this lies with the powerful psychological technique of “acting as if.” If one wishes to meditate, one often begins by acting as if one were meditating. As the form of meditation is assumed, the content of meditation begins to be experienced. If one acts as if one loves another, and acts with sincerity and with honesty, often feelings of love toward that person do begin to flow. So with yoga. If one watches and practices as if yoga were an art and the Self were the artist, then this lends validity to the possibility of this being true.

This brings one to the question of technique. Yoga is also a concrete way of bringing about profound physical, mental, emotional, and spiritual changes within the individual. The student is instructed in yama and niyama (basic ethical precepts), in asanas (meditative postures), in pranayama (conscious voluntary control of the energy associated with breath), in pratyhara and dharana (watching and observing the mind), and finally, in dhyana, or meditation. The final experiencing of samadhi (union) cannot be taught; it is both the result of the foregoing disciplines and independent of them. The understanding of this apparent paradox is part of the process of learning about yoga.

Thus yoga can be seen from the angles of science, art or technique. It can be approached as a small practice of one’s life, or as the way that life is lived, a quality which is brought to live which liberates one from the strong bonds of ego.

Grok AI Predicts TRUMP Crypto to Zero as Democrats Turn the US Blue

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts a price target for the TRUMP crypto memecoin that would see the President’s token utterly worthless

The Official TRUMP memecoin is positioned for a severe and sustained collapse, Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts. In the 2026 midterms, Democrats delivered a sweeping victory, reclaiming control of both the House and the Senate. The party flipped multiple Republican-held Senate seats, including in traditionally red or competitive states, and secured a clear House majority.

This outcome sharply rebukes the current Republican administration and significantly weakens the party’s legislative influence for the remainder of the presidential term. With Democrats now holding both chambers, Republicans’ ability to advance their agenda is heavily constrained, and the balance of power in Washington has shifted decisively.

(SOURCE: Grok AI Predicts a Catastrophic TRUMP Crypto Crash)

Democratic majorities have historically favored stronger regulatory oversight of digital assets. A more assertive stance on crypto, ranging from expanded enforcement to potentially restrictive legislation, would remove much of the political tailwind that previously supported Trump-branded tokens.

Political memecoins like TRUMP are especially vulnerable: their value is tightly linked to narrative momentum and perceived political relevance. A cooler regulatory climate and diminished Republican influence could accelerate capital flight from these assets and pressure the more speculative corners of the broader crypto market.

TRUMP has already experienced a dramatic drawdown from its early 2025 peak near $75 and currently trades at a small fraction of that level, down -15% on the day and -98% from that $75 all-time high.

Price action shows persistent lower highs, weak follow-through on any short-term rallies, and elevated sensitivity to political headlines, sparking fear among the remaining TRUMP crypto holders.

Volume has tended to expand more on downside moves than on recoveries, a classic sign of distribution. With a large portion of the supply still subject to unlocks and concentrated holdings, the technical structure remains fragile.

A break of recent support levels would likely trigger further liquidation and accelerate the downtrend. With reduced political relevance, rising regulatory pressure, and deteriorating technicals, the path of least resistance points sharply lower.

Among all crypto assets, the Official TRUMP memecoin stands out as uniquely exposed. Its value is closely tied to political momentum and the current administration’s standing.

As the most visible Trump-branded token, it is expected to attract disproportionate attention from regulators, critics, and risk-averse investors. While the broader market may feel some pressure, TRUMP is positioned to suffer more severe and prolonged selling than most other tokens.

By January 1, 2027, Official TRUMP could trade in the $0.08 – $0.25 range, with a severe downside scenario testing levels below $0.05.

XRP Casino Transfers: Destination Tags, Transaction Costs, and Common Mistakes

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

At many crypto casino cashiers, XRP has earned itself a distinguished place. It’s a popular way to manage transfers, which settle in seconds and cost virtually nothing, at least from the platform’s perspective. Sounds good, right?

But there are features of XRP that tend to confuse and dismay unsuspecting users. Because most casinos’ deposits require a destination tag, there are nuanced elements that require additional consideration. We’ll take a look at how to use XRP casino transfers and under what conditions they might be right for you.

Running on the XRP Ledger, a network designed for fast and low-cost payments, XRP transactions are ideal for the casino setting because they are fast and relatively forgiving from a fee perspective. Since crypto casinos exist primarily to be affordable, fast ways to move money, these are definitely pluses.

Your deposits appear fast, and you can start playing at the casino without the lengthy wait that might otherwise follow from certain cryptocurrencies or traditional currencies.

And because other cryptocurrencies charge fees that could easily come to $10 to $15, the much more modest fee structure of XRP is often an inviting alternative. If you’re comparing XRP with stablecoin payment options, USDT Casinos is another resource to explore, keeping deposit networks, withdrawal fees, and cashier requirements in mind.

As mentioned in the introduction, most casinos will require a shared XRP address to receive deposits. The destination tag is what tells the casino which account should receive payment.

You could think of the address as an apartment building, while the tag is the apartment number. The payment itself might reach the building, but without that apartment number, what good is it really doing anyone?

Destination tags on this platform are always presented as numbers and can be up to 10 digits long. They will sometimes be expressed as memos or memo IDs by the casino.

Note also that XRP addresses always begin with the letter R. These types of details sound small, but can be genuinely impactful for players trying to move money.

Beginning at the casino cashier, you’ll peruse the drop-down menu looking for XRP as your preferred payment method. Naturally, not every casino will be able to facilitate these types of transactions, but we have included a list of ones that do, presented in a table within this article.

Once you’ve chosen this as your method, the casino will display a deposit address, along with a destination tag. Your job is to copy the values as they’re presented on the screen exactly.

Before you copy those, it’s good to compare the tag one more time against the cashier page, because a single wrong digit can and will completely spoil the transaction. Lost deposits in the world of crypto payments are almost never recoverable, so it’s important to get things right.

Withdrawals are essentially just a reversal of the process described above. Personal wallets like Xaman will typically not require a tag, as the address itself can identify your account. However, an exchange account will almost always require a destination tag.

Copy the tag from the exchange’s XRP deposit page, and then enter it within the casino withdrawal. Casino processing will play a role too. It’s important to understand what they have to do on their end to get things rolling.

This can include identity checks and manual reviews that may make the transaction take longer. These are particularly common for newer accounts.

XRP transaction costs are often just a fraction of a penny, though the actual number may rise and fall a bit depending on circumstances, usually related to network traffic. In either case, it’s still going to be a much cheaper option than the majority of alternative coins.

Note that every XRP Ledger account must hold a minimum reserve to function. The base reserve at the time of writing is one XRP. Short of this figure, you’ll be unable to use an account. So it’s important to maintain this reserve.

Is XRP worth your time and attention? An increasingly large number of people are answering that question with an affirmative. That doesn’t mean it’s right for you, but it does mean it has a good set of features that are well tailored to the needs of many online casino players.

It’s important to remember that when it comes to online casino crypto transactions, investment value is not the consideration. You’re looking for a coin that is fast and forgiving in terms of fee structure. You get both things with XRP.

Why the Spanish Squat Can Help Build Quads without Using a Barbell

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

The traditional back squat has long been the staple of those who want to crush their quads, but standing under a heavy barbell is not for everyone. Mature gym goers, or anyone with mobility issues, may find that the downward force of carrying a heavy bar across the shoulders leads to stress on the spine. Fortunately, IFBB Pro and physique transformation coach, Tanya Johansen swears by the Spanish squat.

“Try a Spanish squat,” recommends the Bikini Pro competitor. “It fries your quads… when done correctly.”

The Spanish squat is a popular movement utilized for rehabbing the knees, but it also pounds the quadriceps, providing the perfect environment for muscle growth. To try it for yourself, stand in front of a cable station or power rack and anchor a resistance band around the support, looping around the back of your knees. You can stand inside a single loop, or loop the band around each knee, as demonstrated by Johansen.

Next, move back a little to make sure there is tension on the band. Now, you should be able to squat by sitting back with the band keeping you upright. Keep your shins vertical, and place emphasis on the quads as you lift and lower your body.  “Band behind your knees, sit back into it, stay tall, let your knees come forward,” summarized the coach, who specializes in physiques that are over 40.

Because there is no barbell compressing your body, the Spanish squat can be executed without overloading the back and knee joints. The quads can still be worked hard with no additional weight, and for those looking to improve their range of mobility, Spanish squats are awesome for building knee strength because they rehab the patellar and its related tendons.

“Over 40? Even better reason,” advises the coach of the Spanish squat. “You don’t need a heavy bar on your back to grow your legs. Forget heavy. Make the muscle do the work.”  And, for those who use the barbell as a crux for their ego, this IFBB Pro has some real talk. “You’re not in there to impress anyone,” she explained. “You’re in there to make sure your quads earn it.”

To make this move more challenging, you can bring in an isometric hold at the top of the squat, pausing before descending back down. You can also slow down the lowering phase for additional time under tension. Aim for 3 sets of 8 to 12 reps.

“It’s not the weight. It’s the work,” concluded Johansen, who’s own physique is proof positive that training smart can bring serious progress.

The Siri AI waitlist is gone in iOS 27: How to try the beta today

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

The wait is over for trying Siri AI on iOS 27. Here's what you need to know to get started.

Enabling Siri AI, Apple’s new generative AI-powered virtual assistant, on a compatible Apple device running iOS 27 used to prompt a message informing the user that the device would be added to a waitlist with varying wait times. Now, you can download Siri AI automatically as soon as you enable it on your compatible iPhone, Mac, or iPad. The one exception is that, if there is unusually high demand, Apple could temporarily reinstate its waitlist.

You’ll need a compatible device that runs Apple Intelligence, like an iPhone (15 Pro or newer), iPad (Mini A17 Pro or newer or an M-series chip), Mac (MacBook Neo or M-series chip), Apple Watch (Series 9 and Ultra 2 or newer), or Apple Vision Pro.

Instead of adding you to a waitlist, Siri AI will begin downloading right away.

Siri AI is Apple’s upgraded virtual assistant, now powered by generative AI. Instead of giving you canned responses to your questions, Siri AI will be able to answer complex questions, understand more natural-sounding phrases, and maintain context throughout conversations and across your Apple ecosystem.

ZDNET’s Lance Whitney recommends using Siri AI’s onscreen awareness feature to ask it for directions, explain something you see on your phone’s screen, or to add items from your screen to a list. He also uses Siri AI to edit his photos simply by asking for the changes he wants. But that’s just skimming the surface. You can read more about Whitney’s experiences using Siri AI here.

Also: I thought I had Siri AI in iOS 27 – until I found it hidden in my settings

For years, Apple has been working to gain relevance in AI to compete with major players like OpenAI and Google. While Apple Intelligence features like Writing Tools, Genmoji, and Image Playground have helped, Apple has lagged far behind in upgrading its signature voice assistant to generative AI.

iOS 27 delivered Siri AI in beta this fall, along with iPadOS 27, MacOS 27 Golden Gate, VisionOS 27, and WatchOS 27.

You need a compatible iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple Watch, or Apple Vision Pro to use Apple Intelligence features like Siri AI. Not all Apple devices that support the latest iOS 27 (or other OS 27) will support Siri AI.

Here are the Apple devices that support Siri AI:

You also need to have your device in a supported language (English, French, Japanese, Korean, Portuguese, or Spanish) and to reside in a supported region (not available in mainland China or in iOS, iPadOS, or WatchOS in the European Union).

How Kimi Antonelli Trains His Brain for Peak Speed—and an F1 Record

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

It’s official, 20-year-old Kimi Antonelli has secured his spot as the youngest F1 Champion in history after just two seasons, but while the dedicated Mercedes driver has been relentless on the track, the speedy Italian has also stayed active outside of his car. Here’s why the history maker’s appetite for adventure is the reason that he never stays still, and how a smart approach to staying active benefits both body and mind.

Kimi Antonelli’s third place in Mexico City on November 1 meant that he’d earned enough points to stave off all other drivers to complete the season as champion, but this overnight success is the result of a mature approach to staying sharp. “I try to do things that keep me active, but won’t tire me out,” explained Antonelli during a recent and telling interview with GQ. “It’s all a way to release mental stress, to just relax and enjoy. I try to do these activities as much as possible because in sports, the mental [part] is basically what does everything.”

The activities that Antonelli spoke of include race related training such as simulators, go-karts, and operating remote controlled cars, but he’s also a keen proponent of padel. Not only has the young upstart played against fellow Mercedes drivers like George Russell, but he’s even been known to stroke the ball from the deck of a super yacht from time to time.

Antonelli’s fuel for dominating F1 was on show early, as he transitioned from Formula 2 and continued his approach to “hard” training, sharing that a day of working out included “An early morning swim, neck training at lunch, and an afternoon bike ride.”

The inspirational Italian is also hot with the hoops, and in a recent Instagram Reel revealed that he can make a basket from an impressive distance, all while standing on one leg!

While Antonelli’s love of fun activities helps him keep a positive mindset as he works towards pole position, science has also shown that becoming skilled in a variety of sporting activities can only help with his day job. Activities such as shooting baskets, stroking the Padel ball, and other loves such as surfing, no doubt boost his reaction times. In fact, a scientific study in 2023 confirmed that reaction training (they used flashing lights as the stimuli) induced beneficial effects on physiological and cognitive performance.

At just 20 years of age, Kimi Antonelli has the world at his highly successful feet. So, what’s next? “Right now, my main goal is to give everything to F1, but in my normal life, I want to be successful, too,” he revealed to GQ. “I want to be efficient, and in 20 to 30 years’ time, when I won’t be racing anymore, I want to still do something that keeps me active. Because once you finish, it’s easy to do nothing after working hard for so many years. I want to be successful outside of racing.”

With an attitude like Antonelli’s, the journey is far from over.

meet the wienermobile, the 1930s quirky pedal car that put hot dogs on wheels

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

from its first appearance on american roads in 1936 to its miniature interpretations by bird corporation and konek, the oversized hot dog has become an enduring piece of advertising and automotive culture.

First introduced in 1936, the Oscar Mayer Wienermobile began as a traveling advertisement shaped like an oversized hot dog. Over the decades, the vehicle has gone through several redesigns, but its distinctive silhouette has remained central to the identity of the brand, turning an unconventional marketing idea into a familiar sight on American roads. Its recognizable form has also inspired toys, merchandise, and unusual experiences. In 2019, Oscar Mayer even transformed a full-size Wienermobile into a one-night Airbnb stay in Chicago, inviting guests to eat, live, and sleep inside the eight-meter-long vehicle (find designboom’s previous coverage here).

Now, two vintage Oscar Mayer Wienermobile pedal cars, produced by Bird Corporation and Konek Inc., are appearing together at auction on Bring a Trailer. Offered as a single lot without a reserve, the two new-old-stock examples are described as having never been ridden by children.

The story of the Wienermobile begins in 1936, when Carl G. Mayer, nephew of Oscar Mayer founder Oscar F. Mayer, proposed a hot dog-shaped vehicle as a traveling advertisement for the company’s products. Built by Chicago’s General Body Company, the original measured approximately four meters long and featured a metal body shaped like an oversized wiener, mounted on wheels. Its two open cockpits accommodated a driver and Little Oscar, the promotional character of the brand.

As the vehicle became an established part of Oscar Mayer’s advertising, successive generations introduced new interpretations of the original concept. The 1958 model, designed by industrial designer Brooks Stevens, introduced a rounded, bubble-like front and more integrated bun-shaped elements. Later versions experimented with larger bodies, updated chassis, and elaborate interiors, always retaining the unmistakable hot dog silhouette.

Oscar Mayer discontinued the program in the late 1970s, shifting its attention toward television advertising. Public enthusiasm for the vehicle, however, prompted its return in 1986 for the company’s 50th anniversary. New generations followed, and the Wienermobile resumed its travels across the United States, appearing at parades, public gatherings, supermarkets, and promotional events.

Over the years, the hot dog on wheels inspired toys, merchandise, and other branded interpretations, translating its instantly recognizable shape into objects that could be collected and played with. The two pedal cars now offered at auction on Bring a Trailer belong to this broader history.

Although they share the same orange-and-yellow color scheme, the Bird Corporation and Konek examples feature different construction methods. The Bird version has a single-piece, hot-dog-shaped plastic body mounted over a yellow base, while the Konek model uses a two-piece orange body with Oscar Mayer and Wienermobile decals across its front, rear, and sides.

Both combine molded plastic exteriors with metal frames, orange wheels, yellow seats, and black steering wheels with yellow horn buttons. Twin foot pedals power the miniature vehicles, while faux headlights, grilles, taillights, and windows complete their automotive appearance. The Konek example measures approximately 114 centimeters long, 53 centimeters wide, and 48 centimeters tall.

Today, the Wienermobile remains part of Oscar Mayer’s identity, with six vehicles operated by parent company Kraft Heinz. Its continued presence on American roads speaks to the unusual longevity of a marketing idea conceived in the 1930s. The hot dog-shaped vehicle has become a recognizable piece of American visual culture and continues to inspire new interpretations nearly 90 years after its first appearance.

The two pedal cars’ appearance at auction offers a chance to revisit the history of the Wienermobile, a promotional vehicle that brought advertising, automotive design, and popular culture together. First introduced in 1936, the hot dog-shaped vehicle has evolved into an enduring fixture of American popular culture.

Sony is enjoying a sharp profit spike in its latest quarterly financials, largely driven by its PlayStation business and the blockbuster success of Spider-Man: Brand New Day. According to the company’s September earnings, Sony’s net profits climbed to 449.60 billion yen (about $2.84 billion) for the three months ended September — a 44 percent increase compared to the 311.4 billion yen ($1.96 billion) it reported for the same period last year.

Spider-Man: Brand New Day was a notable contributor, which released in July and became the third-highest grossing movie in history by earning $2.52 billion at the global box office. That’s a huge boost for Sony Pictures Entertainment, which posted earnings of $581 million for the three months ended September — a 527 percent jump from the $93 million it reported for the same period in 2025.

Sales of Playstation 5 consoles have fallen, with 2.6 million units reported this second quarter compared to the 4 million that were sold in Q2 2025. While the Sony-exclusive Marvel’s Wolverine sold 2.4 million units against a reported budget of $300 million, PlayStation game sales generally have increased for both digital downloads and physical discs. That’s in spite of the week-long player blackout that took place in August to protest Sony’s plan to ditch discs entirely. Looking forward, PlayStation is now looking at its next generation console, which developers are making “solid progress” on according to Sony CFO Lin Tao, and is “progressing as scheduled.”

Consumers are spending on physical gifts even as travel tops wish lists, Visa survey finds

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Consumers are hoping for more travel and experiences this holiday season, but physical gifts continue to win the lion's share of their wallets, according to a new report from Visa on Thursday.

The report found that more than half of survey respondents are putting travel and live events at the top of their wish lists. Still, only 15% of those respondents are prioritizing spending on experiences for others, while 27% of them plan to favor physical gifts.

That gap between what shoppers want and what they plan to buy is largely due to higher travel costs, lower consumer sentiment and an emphasis on value, according to Michael Brown, Visa's principal U.S. economist.

"There is some interesting trade-offs there that we're seeing on the part of consumers," Brown told CNBC. "If I'm traveling more, I'm probably going to be gifting those physical goods less, so this is really the broader context."

The survey was conducted among roughly 4,660 U.S. adults ages 18 and older, designed to be representative of the broader population of consumers.

Brown said budget pressure also factors into traveling for the holidays, as Visa expects more people to celebrate the holidays "a little bit closer to home" than in the past given rising airfares and general inflation. In September, consumer prices rose 3.7% annually, driven higher by surging energy costs.

Airlines have hiked the price of plane tickets this year as their fuel costs have risen by billions of dollars due to the war in the Middle East. And even if fuel prices drop, executives have said fares are likely to stay high, with fares up more than 22% in September compared with a year earlier, according to the consumer price index.

Those trends mean consumers will likely be more intentional about where they're spending, especially as inflation drives up prices across the board.

"Consumers are aspirational. They want those experiences, they want to travel, they want to be out in the world," Brown said. "However, the economic reality is with prices rising, with economic uncertainty, they need to balance the reality of today's economic environment with those aspirations."

The report also found that only households with an income above $200,000 spent more on trips departing between Nov. 1 and early next year compared with 2025 into 2026. On the other hand, many other income groups pulled back on their travel spending.

"If you think about that segment, a lot of them own equities, and the equity market runup over the last several months has created a lot of positivity among that specific affluent demographic, and I think that's one of the factors that continues to motivate even aggregate macro spend today," Brown said.

Meanwhile, less affluent shoppers are placing an emphasis on value. Visa's consumer survey from October showed that roughly one-third of consumers with an income below $100,000 are going to switch to lower-cost brands or stores when shopping for holiday gifts this season.

Though Visa is projecting a 4.5% year-over-year increase in holiday sales growth, much of that figure is due to inflation. Without those price increases, sales are expected to rise just 1.1% this year, down from the 1.3% increase during the 2025 holiday season.

Ultimately, Brown said the survey findings may be encouraging for the broader retail scene this holiday season. With less emphasis on travel and more projected spending on physical gifts, it could boost the sector at a time when spending is under pressure.

At the same time, he said that consumers may just be expecting to spend more this holiday season because they've become accustomed to the higher prices.

"But, of course, consumers are internalizing higher prices of things, and I think that's why they're reporting higher anticipated spend this year," Brown said. "I think these numbers just need to be looked at through the lens of the inflation and particularly consumers' perception of where inflation is going to go. I think that weighed heavily on consumer psychology this season."

Spinach Artichoke Baked Potatoes

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

This baked potato stuffed with spinach-artichoke dip is the ultimate solo comfort meal. Classic twice-baked potatoes require two trips to the oven to deliver a pillowy, gussied-up filling and crispy skins all in one bite. Here, I’ve streamlined the workflow with a microwave. By zapping your spuds to start, you can reduce the overall baking time without sacrificing texture. Pick a large russet (or Idaho) for the best results; their relatively high starch and low moisture content helps them bake up airy and light, not gummy.

The rich, velvety filling that melts over top comes together in moments while your spud cooks. Cream cheese and mozzarella pack in enough dairy richness for a satisfying cheese pull and mellow out the subtle bitterness of baby spinach and nutty tang of artichoke hearts. (It’s important that your cream cheese is room temperature before you mash it in with the veggies. If it’s still got a chill from the fridge, it won’t fully incorporate.) The Worcestershire sauce is optional, but just a few shakes from the bottle bring instant savory depth to the filling.

Most recipes serve multiple people. In Me, Myself, and Dinner, each dish serves one and only one. That's you! Because whether you live solo (almost 30% of Americans do) or are enjoying a night to yourself, sitting down to a meal should be something to look forward to.

Preheat oven to 425°. Poke 1 large russet potato (about 12 oz.) all over with a fork; rub with avocado or vegetable oil to coat and sprinkle with kosher salt. Microwave potato on a microwave-proof plate on high 5 minutes. Turn potato over and microwave on high until fork-tender, about 5 minutes more. Let potato sit until just cool enough to handle.

While potato is cooling, microwave 5 oz. baby spinach with 2 Tbsp. water in a medium microwave-proof glass bowl until wilted, about 2 minutes. Carefully press leaves to release as much liquid as possible, then drain off liquid. Coarsely chop spinach.

Mix 2 Tbsp. cream cheese, room temperature, in a medium bowl until smooth. Add spinach, 4 quartered artichoke hearts (from a 14-oz. can), coarsely chopped, 2 Tbsp. finely grated Parmesan, 2 Tbsp. coarsely grated mozzarella, ½ tsp. garlic powder, and ¼ tsp. Worcestershire sauce (if using) and fold to combine; season with salt and freshly ground pepper.

Slice potato in half lengthwise and lightly fluff flesh in each half. Set on a small foil-lined rimmed baking sheet. Divide spinach mixture between potato halves, piling high (don’t pack too tightly). Sprinkle more Parmesan over. Bake potato halves until very tender when squeezed and skin is wrinkled and crisp, 20–25 minutes. Let cool slightly before eating.

Virgin Atlantic — Cheap Short-Haul Europe in Business

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Business class hops across Europe for fewer points than most domestic economy awards

Picture this: you've just crossed the Atlantic on a long-haul award, touched down at London Heathrow, and now you need to get to Rome for the real adventure to begin. Most travelers at this point resign themselves to a cramped economy seat on a budget carrier, dragging luggage through a secondary terminal. But you? You're walking into the Virgin Atlantic clubhouse, sipping a Bloody Mary, and then settling into a proper business class seat for the 2.5-hour hop to Fiumicino. That's the magic of this redemption. Here's the math that should make your jaw drop. A business class seat on a short-haul European flight — London Heathrow to Rome, London to Athens, London to virtually any major European city — routinely retails for **$1,000 to $3,000 in cash**. Airlines know that business travelers on these routes are often on corporate accounts and will pay whatever it costs. The leisure traveler simply doesn't book business class on a two-hour flight because it feels absurd. And that's exactly the pricing inefficiency the ChooseFI community learns to exploit. Virgin Atlantic's Flying Club prices these short-haul European business class seats at a flat **17,500 miles one-way**. At the low end of that $1,000–$3,000 cash range, you're already extracting **5.7 cents per point** — well above what most programs deliver. At the high end, when those seats spike to $3,000 on peak travel dates, you're looking at a staggering **17.1 cents per point**. For context, most travel rewards experts consider 1.5–2 cents per point a solid redemption. This one can deliver *ten times* that. What makes this feel particularly right for the FI mindset is the positioning angle. This isn't just a standalone splurge — it's the finishing move on a transatlantic trip you've already largely funded with points. You've landed in London. You need to get to your final European destination. Instead of paying $200 for a budget airline seat that charges extra for your carry-on, you drop 17,500 Virgin miles and arrive refreshed, fed, and ready. The opportunity cost of those miles is low; the experiential upgrade is enormous. This is what travel rewards done right looks like: not flying business class because you're wealthy, but because you understood the system better than the airlines expected you to.

The core insight here is understanding how Virgin Atlantic prices its own metal versus partner awards — and then deliberately choosing the right one for the right job.

Virgin Atlantic's Flying Club is famous in the points community for two things. First, its partner award sweet spots: ANA First Class for as low as 55,000–60,000 miles one-way, Delta One at competitive rates. These are the headline acts. But quietly sitting in the same award chart is a genuinely underappreciated gem — Virgin's own short-haul European business class, priced at a flat 17,500 miles regardless of the specific route within that distance band.

Why does Virgin price it this way? Virgin Atlantic operates as a premium carrier and sets its award chart to reflect aspirational redemptions — the kind that make Flying Club membership feel worthwhile. For long-haul routes on their own metal, their pricing is competitive but not exceptional. For short-haul Europe, however, the fixed pricing creates an opportunity: a two-hour flight to Rome or Athens costs the same 17,500 miles as a cheaper, shorter hop, but those premium routes have cash prices that routinely spike into the thousands. The chart wasn't built to be a loophole — it's just that cash prices for short-haul business class in Europe are genuinely volatile and often extraordinary.

The fuel surcharge reality check. Here's where you need to pay close attention. Virgin Atlantic is known for passing fuel surcharges onto award redemptions on their own flights. This is the opposite of what happens on ANA or Delta partner awards booked through Flying Club (which carry zero surcharges). On a short-haul European route, those surcharges are real but typically far more manageable than on a transatlantic segment — often in the $50–$150 range rather than $500+. The CPP math still works strongly in your favor, but you should go in with eyes open: this is not a zero-cash-outlay redemption.

The transfer path is exceptional. This is where the ChooseFI community has a serious structural advantage. Virgin Atlantic Flying Club accepts transfers from essentially every major points currency:

Every major transferable currency gets you there at a 1:1 ratio with instant speed. That means if you're holding Chase points from your Sapphire Reserve or Preferred, Amex points from your Gold or Platinum, or Citi points from your Premier card, you have a direct, frictionless pipeline to 17,500 Virgin miles within minutes of finding availability. This is critical because, as you'll learn in the booking section, Virgin availability on its own short-haul European routes can be limited, and you want to be able to move fast when a seat opens up.

The booking window opens 331 days in advance — use it. Miles don't expire as long as you have account activity within 36 months, so there's no pressure to burn them before you're ready.

Before you touch a single website, clarify your mission: you're looking for Virgin Atlantic-operated business class availability on short-haul European routes departing from London Heathrow (LHR). This is not a search for partner award space — this is VS metal, VS availability, VS seats. Your most common target routes will be LHR → FCO (Rome), LHR → ATH (Athens), and similar European city pairs. Build your itinerary around where VS actually flies in Europe, not every possible destination.

Go to virginatlantic.com and use the award search tool. Select "Book with miles" and enter your route. Virgin shows its own availability directly here — you don't need a third-party search engine for this specific redemption. Search one-way rather than round-trip so you can see each direction independently. If your target dates show no availability, flex by ±3 days. Business class award seats on short-haul routes are typically released in small batches.

Open a second browser tab and search the same route and date on Google Flights or directly on virginatlantic.com for a revenue ticket. Note the cash price for business class. This is your CPP sanity check — if the cash price is $1,200 and you're spending 17,500 miles plus ~$100 in surcharges, you're at roughly 6.3 cents per point. If the cash price is $2,500, your CPP jumps dramatically. This step helps you prioritize which dates to target.

Log into your Virgin Atlantic Flying Club account. If you don't have 17,500 miles already, check your transferable points balances:

Do NOT transfer points until you have confirmed award availability on your target date. Transfers to Virgin are one-directional and cannot be reversed.

Once you've confirmed a seat is available, transfer your points from your chosen program. For Chase, Amex, Citi, and Bilt transfers, the miles land in your Flying Club account instantly. Refresh your Flying Club account, confirm the miles are there, and complete the booking before the seat disappears. Enter your payment details for the taxes and surcharges (typically $50–$150 on short-haul European routes).

If you're struggling to find seats close to your travel dates, shift your strategy to the booking window's leading edge. Virgin opens award availability 331 days in advance. Set a calendar reminder 331 days before your ideal travel date, log in at that exact time, and book the moment inventory releases. Peak-season seats to popular destinations like Rome and Athens move quickly.

After booking, screenshot your confirmation, note your booking reference, and verify the routing, cabin class, and surcharge amounts. Add the flight to your calendar with the Virgin Atlantic booking reference. Sign up for flight notifications.

Virgin Atlantic operates its own short-haul flights from London Heathrow to destinations across Europe including Amsterdam, Paris, Edinburgh, Geneva, and Athens. These are different from partner codeshare flights. The 17,500-point price applies specifically to Virgin Atlantic-operated short-haul business class. Partner flights (booked through Flying Club but operated by Delta, KLM, or Air France) use a different award chart and typically cost significantly more. When searching, filter for Virgin Atlantic flight numbers (VS prefix) to find the sweet spot pricing. The business class product on short-haul includes a dedicated cabin, lounge access at Heathrow, priority boarding, and meal service.

Let's walk through a real scenario that illustrates exactly what this redemption looks like in practice.

The Setup: You've just arrived at London Heathrow on a transatlantic award — perhaps an ANA First Class redemption through the very same Flying Club program. You have a few days in London, and now you need to get to Athens, Greece, where your Mediterranean adventure begins. You check flights: a business class seat on Virgin Atlantic's LHR → ATH route on your target date is priced at $2,200 cash.

The Redemption: You log into virginatlantic.com and find a business class award seat on that same flight: 17,500 Virgin miles + approximately $120 in taxes and fuel surcharges. You have 20,000 Citi ThankYou Points sitting in your ThankYou account from your Citi Premier card. You initiate the transfer — 1:1, instant — and within minutes, your Flying Club account reflects 17,500 miles (with 2,500 to spare for future use). You complete the booking.

The Experience: On the day of travel, you access the Virgin Clubhouse at Heathrow — one of the most celebrated airport lounges in the world. The cocktail bar, the à la carte dining, the spa treatments available before departure — this is the part that makes the ChooseFI community members shake their heads in disbelief when they first experience it. Then you board and settle into Virgin's short-haul business class: a proper dedicated seat, a full meal service, attentive cabin crew. For a two-and-a-half-hour flight, it's a genuinely elevated experience.

You land in Athens refreshed, fed, and without having spent thousands of dollars. That's the positioning flight done right — and it's what separates travelers who understand the travel rewards system from those who are still paying cash for European business class.

Avoid this: Transferring points before confirming availability This is the single most common mistake. Transferring miles to Virgin Atlantic is a one-way street. Avoid this: Confusing this with partner award bookings Virgin's zero-surcharge policy applies to partner airlines like ANA and Delta. Do this: Hold Virgin miles for multiple redemptions Because miles don't expire with account activity every 36 months, there's no reason to hoard and rush. Keep Flying Club active by earning miles through their shopping portal or partners, and wait for the right route and cash price moment to pounce.

This redemption is built for a specific type of ChooseFI traveler: the intermediate-to-advanced points strategist who thinks in itineraries, not individual flights.

The ideal candidate is someone who's already planning a trip that routes through London Heathrow — perhaps on a transatlantic award — and needs an elegant, high-value way to reach their final European destination. Solo travelers and couples benefit most here. At 17,500 miles per person, a couple needs 35,000 Virgin miles, which is completely achievable with a single transfer from a combined Chase or Amex balance.

Families should approach with caution. This redemption scores a 2/5 for family-friendliness for good reason. Multiplying 17,500 miles per person across four or more travelers creates a significant points requirement, and availability for multiple seats on the same flight is far from guaranteed. The fuel surcharges also multiply with passenger count. Families are better served by other redemption strategies.

This is NOT for beginners. The overall score of 2.7/5 and beginner-friendliness rating of 1/5 signal real complexity. If you're just starting your travel rewards journey, build your foundation with simpler redemptions first. But if you've got a year of points accumulation behind you and you're ready to think architecturally about trip planning, this sweet spot can be a revelatory experience.

This redemption is rated **Hard** — and that rating is earned honestly. The difficulty isn't in the math or the concept; it's in three very real execution challenges. **Challenge 1: Finding award availability.** Virgin Atlantic doesn't release enormous quantities of business class award seats on its own short-haul European flights. You may search a dozen dates before finding a seat. This is the primary friction point. The fix: use the 331-day advance booking window religiously, and be genuinely flexible on your travel dates by at least ±3 days. **Challenge 2: Navigating the Virgin Atlantic booking interface.** Compared to programs like Chase's travel portal, Flying Club's award search tool is clunkier and less intuitive. It occasionally shows availability that isn't actually bookable through to completion. The fix: search virginatlantic.com directly, be patient with the interface, and have your payment information ready to move quickly when a seat surfaces. **Challenge 3: Understanding the surcharge reality.** New points enthusiasts sometimes expect zero out-of-pocket on award bookings. Virgin's fuel surcharges on its own metal are real, and they'll surprise you if you're not expecting them. The fix: budget $50–$150 per person in cash costs as a baseline assumption. The good news: the **transfer path is as easy as it gets** in the points world. Instant 1:1 transfers from Chase, Amex, Citi, and Bilt mean that once you find availability, you can have miles in your account and a booking confirmed in under 10 minutes. If you're willing to invest the search time and approach with patience, this is one of those redemptions that fundamentally changes how you see the travel rewards system. The payoff — business class in Europe for 17,500 miles — is worth the effort.

This redemption works best as part of a larger trip. If you are flying transatlantic to or from Europe, route through London Heathrow. Then use the short-haul award to connect to your final European destination at a fraction of the cost of a separate ticket.

Log into VirginAtlantic.com and search for reward flights in Upper Class (business) on your desired European route. Look for VS-coded flights only. Ignore partner-operated results, which will price differently.

Midweek flights (Tuesday through Thursday) and shoulder season dates offer the best availability. Peak summer weekends and holiday periods are harder to find. Build flexibility into your schedule if possible.

Chase Ultimate Rewards transfers to Flying Club are instant, making Chase the preferred transfer partner for time-sensitive bookings. Amex Membership Rewards also transfers 1:1 but may take 1-2 days.

Complete the booking on VirginAtlantic.com. Your business class ticket includes access to the Virgin Atlantic Clubhouse at Heathrow, one of the best airport lounges in Europe. Arrive early and enjoy it as part of the experience.

Virgin Atlantic operates short-haul flights from London Heathrow to destinations including Amsterdam, Paris CDG, Edinburgh, Geneva, Athens, Tel Aviv, and several others. Routes change seasonally, so check VirginAtlantic.com for current schedules. The 17,500-point sweet spot applies to all Virgin Atlantic-operated short-haul business class flights.

Yes, Virgin Atlantic Flying Club prices awards as one-way, so you can book a single short-haul business class segment for 17,500 points. This makes it ideal for positioning flights where you only need one direction covered by points.

The flight itself is short, but the value extends beyond the seat. You get dedicated check-in, priority boarding, the Heathrow Clubhouse lounge (which is excellent), a meal service, and a more relaxed travel experience. At 17,500 points, the per-point value (3-5 cents) makes it a strong redemption even for a short flight.

Virgin Atlantic does add taxes and fees to award tickets, including some carrier-imposed surcharges. On short-haul European routes, total out-of-pocket costs typically run $50-$150 depending on the route and departure airport taxes. This is higher than some programs but still reasonable given the business class product.

The Virgin Atlantic short-haul European business class sweet spot is the perfect complement to a transatlantic award trip. At just 17,500 points, it costs less than most domestic economy awards while delivering business class service, Heathrow Clubhouse lounge access, and a stress-free connection to destinations across Europe. It is not the flashiest redemption in travel rewards, but it is one of the most practical: a building block that makes multi-city European itineraries accessible and affordable for the FI community.

New Food Pyramid Puts Healthy Carbs and Plant-Based Foods First

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

A first-of-its-kind international food pyramid, developed by 22 leading nutritionists from Europe, Canada, the United States, and Australia, was published in Advances in Nutrition in September.

This new food pyramid emphasizes plant-based protein sources, cooking with plant-based oils, and healthier sugars and carbohydrates.

It also echoes the traditional food pyramid used in the United States since 1992.

The new pyramid breaks away from the Trump administration’s updated nutritional guidelines, which are part of Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr.’s Make America Health Again (MAHA) agenda and prioritize red meat over plants.

The new pyramid was designed by a research team led by Brigham Young University professor Karen Della Corte. The researchers noted that this is the first international food pyramid that is designed to reflect scientific agreement across multiple countries and cultures.

Della Corte and researchers from the International Carbohydrate Quality Consortium (ICQC), compared dietary guidelines from multiple countries, including:

They also analyzed evidence from more than 250 scientific publications, while drawing on the EAT-Lancet Planetary Health Diet to identify principles of healthy eating that may be shared.

The new pyramid highlights three guidelines:

While the pyramid emphasizes plant-based proteins, it doesn’t exclude animal proteins.

“I find the new international food pyramid a more practical and aligned approach for the prevention of chronic disease,” said Michelle Routhenstein, Preventive Cardiology Dietitian at Entirely Nourished, who wasn’t involved in the creation of the food pyramid.

“The new international food pyramid places greater emphasis on food quality and the importance of building meals around nutrient-dense plant foods, while still allowing room for lean animal proteins such as fish, poultry, eggs, and yogurt,” she added.

While the new pyramid and the current U.S. dietary guidelines differ in many ways, they also agree on certain factors.

For example, both sets of guidelines discourage added sugars, refined carbs, and highly processed snacks.

The differences between the two mainly lie in their emphasis on specific sources of protein and fat. For example, the new pyramid prioritizes plant-based proteins, while the U.S. guidelines place greater emphasis on animal proteins, including red meat.

While both sets of recommendations advise limiting saturated fat, the new pyramid emphasizes unsaturated plant oils, whereas the U.S. guidelines also include butter and beef tallow as cooking fat options.

Routhenstein noted that an important distinction is that this new pyramid goes beyond simply telling people to eat more protein or fewer carbohydrates. Instead, it encourages people to consider the quality and sources of those nutrients.

However, the main emphasis of both guidelines is encouraging people to eat fewer ultra-processed foods and more natural or “real” foods.

Routhenstein told Healthline that research consistently shows that plant-forward eating patterns, including the Mediterranean and DASH diets, are associated with better cardiovascular health and a lower risk of heart disease.

Plant foods provide fiber, potassium, antioxidants, and other beneficial compounds that can help support healthy cholesterol levels, blood pressure, blood sugar, and blood vessel function.

“This is not to say that protein and fat are unimportant, but their sources matter,” Routhenstein said.

In a statement from BYU, Della Corte shared the following tips for incorporating the new pyramid’s recommendations into your diet:

The pyramid is a framework, not a personalized prescription. If you have questions about your individual nutritional needs, you can speak with your healthcare professional or a licensed dietitian.

“It is important to recognize that plant-forward does not mean vegan or vegetarian. The amount and sources of animal protein should be considered to ensure they align with an individual’s cardiometabolic goals and lab targets,” Routhenstein said.

Liam Fitzgerald, a popular online coach and fitness influencer has passed away at just 29 years of age after injuries sustained during a fatal motorcycle accident. Tributes have poured in for the star, and his family have hailed him as an inspirational figure.

Fitzgerald, who was born in Evansville, Wisconsin, and was a social media influencer and OnlyFans creator, died on October 30, per announcement by his girlfriend Jamie Lyn Rinaldi. “It is with the heaviest heart that I share the devastating news that Liam passed away following a motorcycle accident on October 30th at approximately 2 AM,” she wrote via Instagram. “He suffered severe injuries, and despite the efforts of those who tried desperately to save him, they were unable to bring him back. But he always strived to grow and do better. He had dreams, ambitions, and so much more life to live. He had just turned 29. So many places to see, memories to make, and people to love. His life was tragically cut short in a motorcycle accident, leaving behind a heartbreak that words will never be able to describe.”

Arsenal injury latest: Konsa concern, Saliba return and Saka update

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal will hope Ezri Konsa recovers in time for Saturday’s 3pm Premier League clash with Hull City after he came off during their Champions League win over Slavia Prague.The return of William Saliba to the matchday squad is a great boost for Mikel Arteta ahead of the game, but Bukayo Saka remains out as he continues to recover from his quad injury.

Here is all the latest Arsenal injury news and when the three players are expected to return.

Konsa started Arsenal’s 2-1 victory over Slavia Prague on Wednesday but came off in the second half after experiencing discomfort.

He appeared to limp during the first half, so Arsenal will monitor his fitness closely ahead of Saturday’s match.

Arteta said the change was not a tactical one, as Konsa had been playing a lot of games recently and had some problems during the match.

The Arsenal boss is likely to give an update ahead of Saturday’s game depending on how he recovers in the coming days.

Konsa’s absence will leave Arsenal without as many reliable centre backs. However, Gabriel will play a key role in the backline, and Arteta has other options in Cristhian Mosquera and Piero Hincapie.

Potential return date: November 7 vs Hull City (3pm kick-off).

A positive from the Gunners’ trip to Prague was the inclusion of Saliba in the squad.

The defender has not played this season after picking up a long-term back injury. The issue got worse during his time with France at the World Cup finals, but he managed to avoid surgery.

Saliba has steadily built up his workload and played for 45 minutes in a behind-closed-moors game. He returned to full training on Tuesday and travelled with the squad to take on Slavia Prague.

Although he did not come off the bench, his inclusion shows he is getting close to being back in contention.

Arteta said that Saliba was feeling confident, but it will depend on the needs of the squad.

It gives Arsenal an extra experienced centre-back, and if Konsa is not available against Hull, then he could make an appearance on the pitch.

Potential return date: November 7 vs Hull City (3pm kick-off).

Saka is currently the main attacking concern for Arsenal after he has been sidelined with a quad injury for the last four games.

The winger suffered the injury approximately 10 minutes into the Arsenal Champions League clash against Bayern Munich last month.

The original fears were that Saka would be out for up to eight weeks. Arsenal have not given any confirmation on how long it will take him to make a full recovery.

He will need to undergo another scan to get a better idea of the amount of swelling and fluid in the area. The medical staff will also want to see if the tendon is involved.

This means it is unlikely he plays against Hull. Arsenal are not expected to bring him back too quickly ahead of the international break.

Arteta will miss one of his most influential attackers. Hopefully the next scan will give a clearer indication of how long he is likely to be out for.

Expected return date: Unknown. Saka is not expected to face Hull City on November 7.

Arsenal host Hull City at the Emirates Stadium on Saturday, November 7th.

The game comes ahead of a tough sequence of games which sees Manchester City, Real Madrid and Tottenham on the horizon. Arteta will want as many defensive and attacking threats on hand.
